# WoW Forever leveling: browser guide, RestedXP edition and implementation plan

> **Current blocker audit — V1651–V1652:** Full group XP validation cannot advance past the normal Maraudon Scion payout without current-server evidence. The three-turn blocked audit is satisfied. No numerical model or published guide changed. [Required sample and remaining scope](forever-leveling-validation-blockers.md). The successful numerical results below retain their limited scope.

## Current private validation — November 6, V1643–V1650

The private **V1643 candidate** reuses the unchanged **V1592 engine / XP revision 219**. Twelve historical base-component curves now have exact reference evidence; Knife Revealed changes **375 → 380**. Scripted encounters and any additional script XP remain independently unqualified. Physical actions, quantities, combat and global guards are unchanged. [Evidence, saved-state failure and remaining limits](forever-leveling-script-base-reward-references.md).

All **98,460 quest/level checks**, 654 client bindings and 2,596 independently checked historical values pass. Six primary scenarios are freshly replayed; expected lower progress without mallet confirmation is **46 + 48,073**. Fresh accounting covers **60 old/new player forecasts / 141,600 steps**. Reward, saved-continuation, native export and static flow checks pass. The unchanged runtime's 4,352-test suite, typecheck, lint and builds are reused, not rerun.

**92 quests / 2,435 overlevel cells** remain unsupported across the full collection. Only Horn of the Beast remains incomplete in the raw selected route. Normal Scion payout and Twisted Evils' **25 carvings each / 125 per party** single-clear yield remain unresolved. The dense candidate exceeded the saved-state limit; verified redundant-row removal preserves every resolved reward and reduces the model to **9,990 / 10,000 characters**. Further changes require a capacity check. Full 1–60, current payouts, travel/gameplay and wider groups remain unfinished. Public V1394 and canonical locks are unchanged; nothing is committed or deployed. Receipt: `.cache/leveling-no-grind-20261031/source-flow-progress-v1650.json`. Earlier statuses below are historical where superseded here.

## Latest Maraudon routing progress — November 6 local time, V1449–V1466

The private **V1456 route / frozen V1459 engine** adds the complete **3181 → 3182 → 3201** Margol chain after the earlier five-quest routing improvement. It accounts for one shared outdoor elite fight, one starter per player, actual supplied horn/proof items, intrinsic prerequisites and all three recipients. The existing **14,250 reference quest XP** is credited only at those hand-ins. The approach uses the open Badlands route, without requiring the gate key before earning it. [Item lifecycle, route, actual bars and checks](forever-leveling-margol-horn.md).

For the primary five-Human party from 1+0 without manual mallet confirmation, the lower bars are **46 + 849 / 46 + 44,185 / 46 + 84,018** for fast/expected/slow. All six modeled workloads, with and without optional mallet confirmation, now fund Maraudon's level-46 entry without explicit grinding. Each then stops at unresolved normal Scion XP. These workloads are reference calculations, not guarantees of every possible loot outcome. Twisted Evils retains the user-selected **25 carvings per player, 125 for the party**; its full-clear yield remains unqualified.

The changed engine passes **4,203 tests / 250 files**, strict typecheck, full source lint and all six entrypoint builds; all 248 older test files remain unchanged. Independent checks pass **thirty player/workload/tool cases**, **150 saved handoffs / 750 player continuations**, twenty native class/difficulty/role exports, Lua syntax and thirty source cases with zero warnings or violations. Freeze verification checks 25,659 pins. The earlier quest-event prefix is unchanged in all thirty player cases.

Pariah's conversion reference is now confirmed in the local **70205 and 70450** client tables: all five distinct gems and the activation amulet are reagents for one Amulet of Union. The supplied instruction book is not a substitute. Raw hashes, record counts and exact rows pass the separate source review; runtime conversion and physical acquisition still need implementation. [Recipe evidence and remaining method work](forever-leveling-maraudon-methods.md#the-pariahs-instructions--source-review-not-yet-in-the-route).

Full 1–60, the original **528 edition/settings, 32 mixed settings and 784 native starter cases**, current-server payouts and travel optimality remain unproven. Pariah's full method, normal Scion payout and Twisted acquisition are still pending. These drafts are not published; the public **V1394 tablet download** and canonical locks are unchanged. Receipt: `.cache/leveling-no-grind-20261031/source-flow-progress-v1466.json`. Earlier statuses below are historical where superseded here.

## Latest hosted guide — November 5, V1171/V1173

<https://kfcguild.online/tools/forever/leveling/download> now serves the latest independently checked **levels 1–41** prefix through **More Wastewander Justice in Tanaris**, with **1,502 tasks / 60 RestedXP 4.11.27 documents**. It adds **Mudrock Soup and Bugs** at the existing Theramore visit and retains the corrected Faldir's Cove circuit and Bloodsail 604 camp stage. At equal creature levels, the smaller expected kill quota wins. Existing source predicates, quantity/sharing evidence, captured drop rates, the >25% camp rule and +4/+5 safety limits remain enforced. ## Earlier local status — outdoor fallback and consistent handoffs (V392–V409)

The primary party is still the five actual Humans, together from 1 + 0: Warrior tank, Priest healer, Mage, Rogue and Warlock. **Full 1–60 readiness remains false.** No commit, push, deployment, installation or game execution is included in this code-only work.

The optional **30% outdoor allowance** is persisted and replaces the existing 10% outdoors. It estimates additional suitable mobs killed while travelling; it adds no quest rewards, dungeon multiplier, loot or objective credit. ## Earlier reward evidence — Highland Hides correction (V380–V384)

A read-only audit of the current 1,638-fact snapshot verifies **652 client-table facts / 27,589 stored level rows** against independent exact-decimal integer arithmetic and matching captured 70205/70450 records. No stored client curve differs from that arithmetic. Ordinary quests correctly retain their own multiplier, while boosted dungeon records carry their explicit quest multiplier. A blanket additional XP multiplier would be incorrect. This checks the reference calculation, not a controlled current-server payout.

Among 29 comparable, available published final/scaled rewards, 28 match the client calculation. **Scourge of the Downs (3523)** retains its published 680 XP instead of the reference calculation's 675; no published value is silently replaced. V380's initial comparison included two already-conflicted facts as numerical differences; V381 correctly keeps those conflicts held. The 101 remaining historical facts with matching client bindings are class-specific, so they do not justify adding class detours to the shared route.

The audit does expose a real extra-coefficient error for **Highland Hides (98815)**. Its captured published reward and both client records agree on **1,150 full XP**, with client multiplier 1. Listing the quest with a dungeon nevertheless causes the planner's fallback coefficient to raise it to **2,750**. The inactive V382 data correction marks that published amount as a final reward. All original published amounts, level curves, access and evidence kinds remain intact; the other 1,637 facts are unchanged. Published overlevel rounding is retained, rather than relabelled as independently measured client/server behavior. The quest remains selected.

V383 qualifies the same 114/431/832 task payloads on that corrected data: each block passes 48 forecast/save cases and forty native/Lua control exports. In the expected coordinated five-Human profile, the level-30 handoff becomes **30 + 31,952 through 30 + 39,508**, compared with 30 + 33,509 through 30 + 41,115 on the older data. The party still reaches the required handoff without changing its tasks. The total bar difference includes subsequent level-dependent kill XP, so it is not asserted to equal exactly 1,600.

**The local prefix index now selects the corrected V383 references after V387/V388 verification.** The original V382 candidate and its qualification record remain preserved. This updates local prefix evidence, without publishing a website dataset or qualifying the complete guide. V373/V374 use the older snapshot; V387/V388 below provide the corrected-data replay. The original 33 editions / four mixed rosters / sixteen legacy settings / 784 native starter combinations remain required after the primary party.

## Earlier local change — optional outdoor route clearing (V320–V331)

The owner allows a fallback where the party kills additional suitable mobs while travelling. The planner now supports a separately persisted **outdoor travel allowance**, including 30%, in advanced combat settings. It replaces the general allowance outdoors rather than stacking on the existing 10%. Blank inherits the general setting; zero explicitly disables the outdoor allowance. The server kill-XP multiplier, quest rewards, actual recorded bars, kill/item counters and dungeon allowance remain independent. Additional XP is explicitly estimated and extra fighting takes time; this is not free XP, a measured duration or a guarantee of reaching a level.

Campaign **v194 / XP v159** applies the override consistently to shared-party combat, personal phases and material gathering. The independent funding witness checks each row against the correct outdoor/dungeon percentage. It fails before the correction and passes after, with payouts and physical work unchanged.

All eight shards complete with exit 0 and no recorded XP-conservation, duplicate-reward, enemy-limit, forty-slot or restoration differences. The existing 832 approved tasks remain identical. This qualifies the reached numerical comparison, not an accepted production route, optimal travel or full 1–60.

### Desolace coverage audit — November 4

Desolace was partially used, not exhausted. The reached earlier visit already pays Centaur Bounty (1387), Sceptre of Light (5741), Book of the Ancients (6027), Down the Scarlet Path (261/1052), and the opening Karnitol shipwreck work. The complete Vahlarriel/Tyranis chain (1437 → 1465 → 1438 → 1439 → 1440) was instead scheduled at the later Maraudon visit, beyond the current blocker. Selecting it somewhere in the full itinerary did not help the earlier XP gap.

The V317 controlled placement moves the nine existing chain actions to the earlier Desolace visit without adding or duplicating any quest or changing the approved 832-action prefix. Five actual classes across all three loot workloads receive exactly **12,500 reference quest XP/player** once, retain a nine-slot journal peak, and pass the chain's actual +4 encounter checks. Expected progress reaches **35 + 57,622 through 36 + 2,927**; fast reaches 35 + 45,824 and slow 36 + 2,180 at the lower bound. All still stop before full Scarlet Monastery entry 38. This is a placement experiment, not an accepted production route or an optimal travel/native qualification. Its initial V316 harness counted ten actions instead of nine and failed before a forecast; that failed log is retained.

Next review prioritizes Desolace rather than assuming another zone is required: integrate the earlier chain with the existing fortress visit, assess Kodo Roundup (5561), and qualify Ghost-o-plasm Round Up (6134). Ghost-o-plasm's captured loot rate is 100%, with eight pooled items/player (forty for five) and level 38–39 spectres, so the +4 rule requires level 35; its supplied-tool summoning method still needs qualification. Reagents I/II (1458/1459) need multiple independent personal counters; they are not supported by the existing single-item funding parser. Captured Hatefury Trickster rates are approximately 27% for each reagent, while other Hatefury sources are mostly below 25%. Full five-player quantities and overlapping drops must be modeled before judging efficiency. Bone Collector's current-client availability and the centaur reputation branches remain unresolved; they contribute no assumed XP.

These later stages currently sit in `post-scarlet-east`, after the level-38 Scarlet Monastery gate. Their eventual rewards therefore cannot fund that gate. ## Previous work — normal dungeon collections and safe XP funding (V210)

The normal-clear policy now covers existing core dungeon collections as well as the ten owner-selected additions. It does not add other optional quests to the itinerary. Deviate Hides and Researching the Corruption remain exceptions; unreviewed optional quests, deferred stages and class-specific work are not silently promoted. Complete item quotas, source identities, pickup prerequisites, physical enemy limits and actual hand-ins remain required. Extra collection-kill counts, XP and clear duration are not invented.

Three regression cases for existing core quests fail with the old ten-quest whitelist and pass with the correction. Initial test-fixture errors and a shell glob failure are preserved; corrected focused tests, strict types and all checks above pass.

The previous production run also exposed a funding defect: a new combat proposal could replace a finite level gap with unresolved objective work in another loot workload. Every new shared-combat batch now receives the same cross-workload safety checks as moved/reused work. Blocked objective phases count as unsafe, and an unknown replacement at the same checkpoint cannot hide a known deficit. This changes proposal acceptance, not the game's XP formula, drop probabilities or +4/+5 rules. The regression reproduces the old missed objective; a separate real-forecast fixture checks selection of the priced alternative across a level-spanning loot range.

Fall of Dun Modr 472 is an independent required on-road courier stage on the existing Menethil → Dun Modr approach. Its completed source program is retained even when an XP-only branch would otherwise skip it. The subsequent Thandol 631/632 pair remains atomic and subject to its actual minimum level. No new journey, source phase or reward is invented.

Collection checks now reject ordinary non-collection objectives before scanning route metadata. All 898 actual-plan predicate comparisons match the earlier implementation. The isolated 10,000-call rejection benchmark improves from about 2,305 ms to 1.36 ms; this is a helper benchmark, not a claim of equivalent whole-route speedup.

Fresh preparation used the unchanged qualified V209 catalog/combat/reward inputs, five Humans at **1 + 0**, all eight primary settings and a frozen checked runtime. The producer completed after 28 minutes 34 seconds; exit 0 records successful computation, not a complete guide. Its plan has 1,548 actions. The independent audit exited 2 after all 48 workload/save forecasts, 24 enemy audits and twenty native inventories/Lua checks. Forty journal checks pass. There are zero projected-event interval differences, timer problems or priced enemy-cap violations. Eight expected-workload rows remain unresolved, and Highland Hides still lacks four creature levels. All original **33 editions / four mixed rosters / sixteen settings / 784 native starter combinations** remain required.

## Previous local review — loot workloads and combat entry (V197)

| Work | Result | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Combat funding gates | Positive XP shortfalls on actual blocked combat events become funding frontiers | Implemented; a fight's own XP cannot fund entry |
| Workload coverage | Fast, expected and slow loot for each existing configuration | 24 primary / 48 legacy / three starter funding states; every original setting retained |
| Candidate simulation | Uses the same loot workload as its starting bars and bounds | Implemented; regression comparison keys include the workload |
| Nested action IDs | Generated funding can itself need preparation | Bounded, deterministic, collision-checked keys preserve full checkpoint metadata and round-trip through the progress schema |
| Sleeping-bag flow | Final two handoffs on the earliest actual shared Hillsbrad visit after the dam | Implemented; complete timed deliveries before the wall detour, preserve every reward and action identity |
| Tests | 2,716 leveling + 202 web + 47 script tests | 2,965 pass; types, lint and package build pass |
| Fresh replay | Frozen runtime and unchanged V196 catalog/combat/rewards | Failed the connected early-route assertion; no numerical qualification |

The old funder considered pickup minima and explicit checkpoints, but omitted blocked combat floors and priced preparation only with expected loot. That could leave the fast-loot route facing level-32 enemies while still level 27. The regression now proves a fast-only combat shortfall is funded before the encounter even when expected/slow players already qualify; a large reward after that encounter cannot bypass the entry limit. Scenario expansion preserves the original configuration axes and adds workload sensitivity rather than replacing any setting.

A separate regression failed because nesting a generated combat gate could produce action keys longer than the 180-character progress limit. Compact opaque keys fix that case, retain the original checkpoint/source metadata, avoid already-used keys and preserve deterministic output. Normal existing IDs stay unchanged.

The sleeping-bag route now places the wall handoffs on the first eligible actual Hillsbrad quest visit after the dam. It tries a position before the visit, or after a timed hand-in where necessary, and rejects a placement inside an active timed attempt. It ignores earlier-than-dam, optional, personal, ambiguous and inside-dungeon visits. Moving a reward in planning is not an early XP payment: only its newly scheduled real hand-in earns it.

Evidence: `.cache/leveling-no-grind-20261031/verification-v197-checks.json`, `scope-amendment-v197-loot-combat-frontiers.json` and `primary-xp-v197-review/`. Runtime/source hashes are frozen before the live replay. Preserve all V196 receipts and the original **33 editions / four mixed rosters / sixteen settings / 784 native starter combinations**. Do not edit production/source/numerical workers while that replay is live. No commit, push, deployment, installation or in-game execution.

## First completion target — five Human characters

**Human Warrior tank, Priest healer, Mage/Rogue/Warlock DPS**, together from Northshire at level 1 with 0 XP through level 60. The owner's latest instruction excludes class quests and individual class detours throughout this route. All five follow the same ordinary quests while retaining their actual class identities.

An ordinary quest available to every member remains eligible when its item rewards differ by class. Those reward choices are not class-quest restrictions. Dedicated progression chains such as Shaman totems or Warlock demons remain outside the group route; each player chooses their own ordinary quest reward.

Object interactions—boxes, flags, the Westfall compass and other supported world-object collections—remain suitable. Each character must still obtain their required items. Personal mob drops are priced for all five: eight items each at a 10% drop rate means 400 expected kills. Such farms are excluded unless an explicitly reviewed valuable chain justifies them. Worthwhile high-drop tradeable meat may use stock, affordable AH purchases or on-route gathering; Redridge Goulash remains excluded. The [supply contract](forever-leveling-source-collections.md#ordinary-meat-and-ah-supplies--november-2-2026) records that exception.

The baseline retains quest hand-ins, required kill objectives and supported acquisition estimates. Missing extra acquisition XP may be omitted without removing the real collection task or its quantities. Tents/rested/food remain spare margin. No grinding XP, early hand-in reward or weakened prerequisite may fund a gate.

Objective-model labels such as `non-outdoor-zone`, `provided-item-interaction` and `unresolved-objective-text` are not treated as proof of personal ineligibility. Their actual objective/source requirements remain in force. The first implementation supports Human starts; other starting races are explicitly deferred.

## Five-player completion target

Optimize the time until all five players reach level 60 from level 1 + 0 XP. The owner's benchmark is below 48 hours `/played` per player, using efficient elite/dungeon quests, shared objectives and minimal recovery and travel waste. Scouting belongs on existing passes and planned NPC returns. XP tables validate the itinerary; maximizing isolated quest XP is not the optimization objective. The [speed and travel contract](forever-leveling-five-player-speed.md) records the acceptance criteria and required measurements. No complete-run time is verified yet.

## Historical v39: ready hand-ins and pickup-provided deliveries

The current ready-visit compiler collects verified completed source hand-ins before a blocked pickup. The authorized catalog retains 464 pickup-provided item identities with item-export provenance; no NPC, quest objective, loot row, reward, recording or dungeon-clear input was changed.

It first preserves the established quest allocation, then proposes ready visits and rejects the proposal if any supported configuration reaches an earlier blocker or a larger deficit at the same task. It moves a whole active NPC visit within its existing leg/audience, checks every reachable supported configuration, preserves source IDs and class utilities, and admits a provided parcel only when its exact objective names the provided item and friendly recipient. Key-use objectives, collection work, item use, quest-state guards, training and unmodeled utility steps cannot be promoted. Information links are retained.

The shared starter now passes its old level-7 pickup and reaches **8 + 4,506 XP**, still **894 XP short of quest 109's level-9 pickup**.

### Confirmed product input and remaining unknowns

The owner confirms four quests per faction for each applicable newly introduced Forever dungeon.
Treat that bundle count as a fixed, owner-confirmed planning input, not as a probability that the
quests might exist. Preserve that provenance; do not present it as independently collected source
data or measured rewards. Missing IDs must not exclude these dungeons from the planning route.

| Information                            | Planning treatment                                                  | Evidence still needed                                                   |
| -------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Four quests per faction                | Guaranteed bundle input supplied by the owner                       | Independently sourced identities when available                         |
| Known quest IDs and restrictions       | Retain all real quests and evaluate each member's eligibility       | Resolve source/build conflicts rather than erase restrictions           |
| Missing quest IDs                      | Stable internal bundle slots, not fabricated game IDs               | Map slots to real quests without counting both                          |
| Uncollected reward amounts             | Level-appropriate unscaled reference base XP × 2.375, once          | Replace with compatible collected or observed final rewards             |
| Planning quest level                   | Explicit dungeon setting, e.g. the owner's Dalaran level-33 example | Keep separate from entry level, pickup minimum and actual hand-in level |
| Pickups, objectives, access and travel | Retain known instructions; mark unidentified details pending        | Real source details before emitting exact executable steps              |

Four Alliance and four Horde slots are alternative faction bundles, not eight rewards for one
player. They are quest counts, not four guaranteed levels or four independently verified payouts.
Known extra quests remain in the catalog; four is not a cap that deletes real content.
Select the complete four-quest bundle by default when offering the dungeon. Players may skip
quests or the dungeon; a default recommendation is not permission to report unearned rewards.

Real quest-ID arrays for Drowned City, Krol'dok and Alcaz Prison remain empty by design until the
IDs are identified. Their separate guaranteed bundles now contain four selected slots per faction
and are included as planning-only dungeon legs in the Alliance campaign. No fake numeric IDs are
inserted into the quest catalog. Both faction bundle models are implemented; the full executable
campaign compiler still covers Alliance, not Horde.

### Concrete sourced anchor: City of Dalaran

The owner supplied the [City of Dalaran quest listing](https://www.wowhead.com/forever/zone=16544/city-of-dalaran)
as the concrete four-quest example. The eight existing local Wowhead XP captures from October 18
also pass the quest-XP record schema and agree on quest level 33, reference base XP 3,950, page
multiplier 2.375 and final full reward 9,400 per quest:

| Faction  | Real quest IDs             | Full bundle quest XP per eligible character |
| -------- | -------------------------- | ------------------------------------------- |
| Alliance | 92456, 92489, 92457, 92458 | 4 × 9,400 = 37,600                          |
| Horde    | 96984, 96987, 96988, 96986 | 4 × 9,400 = 37,600                          |

These are real identified quests, not unidentified bundle slots. Their final 9,400 values must
take precedence over a recomputed estimate; do not multiply them by 2.375 again. The captured
level-specific rewards still determine the result at other hand-in levels. Quest level 33 must
not be promoted to a minimum pickup level or mandatory dungeon entry level.

The Alliance itinerary now selects all four quests by default, including Starving Arcane 92457,
so its full-bundle forecast includes 37,600 quest XP before hand-in-level reductions. Dungeon
visits and individual quest execution remain player choices: use Next without Done or navigate to
another chapter to leave work unfinished rather than falsely recording it as complete. The guide
must not require another clear merely because an objective remains unfinished. These navigation
choices do not award rewards, update actual XP or satisfy access/prerequisite checks.

The earlier three-quest subtotal of 28,200 is not the default Dalaran bundle. Keep all four quests
in the default campaign/export, while detailed saved trip selections remain explicit player choices.
Forecast rewards describe the chosen plan, not already earned XP; use actual party bars after the
player skips work. Do not infer that quest availability guarantees all objectives in one clear.

Use this as the regression anchor for exact-four faction accounting, real-ID replacement,
selected-versus-full totals and prevention of double scaling. Other dungeon levels must use their
own level-appropriate base rewards; Dalaran's absolute 37,600 is not a universal dungeon total.
The live page required JavaScript verification during this check, so the numeric validation here
uses the existing saved captures rather than claiming a fresh independent live-page extraction.

default premade selection and compiled campaign lifecycle; Thanes' fifth known quest is retained.
The three unidentified dungeons each have four default-selected planning slots per faction.

### Bundle identity, replacement and persistence

- Introduce a small, schema-validated bundle model alongside the existing dungeon and campaign
  models. Keep it separate from public quest/reference records, whose numeric IDs represent real
  game entities. Suggested owning module: `packages/leveling/src/dungeon-bundles.ts`. Apply the
  guarantee to an explicit inventory of newly introduced dungeons, not every classic dungeon.
- Give each slot a deterministic internal key such as `drowned-city:alliance:quest-1`. Record
  dungeon, faction, configured planning quest level, reference pattern, count provenance and an
  optional real quest mapping. Do not generate fake numeric IDs, Wowhead links or `.accept`,
  `.complete` or `.turnin` commands for unidentified slots.
- Reconcile known quests against the guaranteed bundle. With two mapped core quests, create only
  two remaining unidentified slots, not four additional rewards. Preserve extra and restricted
  real quests separately; a class-only reward cannot silently become a reward for every member.
- Require an explicit reviewed mapping when a real ID replaces a slot. Never match by list order
  or reward similarity alone. One real quest pays once per member, even across prerequisites,
  multiple visits and bundle/catalog representations. Faction alternatives can reference a shared
  real quest without awarding it twice to the same character.
- Preserve slot identity, source revision and replacement history in saved plans. A new evidence
  release must not mark a quest accepted/rewarded, erase completion or reinterpret a player's
  stored progress. Validate legacy saves and provide a versioned migration if the schema changes.

### One XP ledger with explicit reward provenance

Use the compatible collected final reward at the relevant hand-in level first, retaining legitimate
zero-XP rewards and existing identity-conflict checks. If no such reward exists, use a reviewed
unscaled base reference for the real quest; for an unidentified slot, use the selected anchor's
level-dependent base-XP pattern at the configured planning quest level. Apply 2.375 once, retaining
per-quest rounding and applicable hand-in-level reductions. A planning level of 33 is not permission
to accept a quest at 33, evidence of a minimum level, or a replacement for the player's actual bar.
Initialize each unidentified bundle from the owner's selected upper dungeon planning level, such
as Dalaran 33, with a per-dungeon override. Do not silently substitute an entry minimum, maximum
enemy level or a different catalog difficulty band's upper bound for that setting.

Never multiply already-adjusted Wowhead rewards again. Do not apply the dungeon coefficient to
outdoor prerequisites, kill XP or the player level-cost curve. Do not copy the same absolute reward
bundle between dungeon levels. A missing base pattern must produce an actionable configuration
error, not zero XP, arbitrary XP or a claim that the guaranteed quests do not exist.

Carry each member's XP continuously through pickups, dungeon work and hand-ins, awarding quest XP
at the planned hand-in rather than at dungeon entry. Expose the known reward subtotal, estimated
reward subtotal and combined planning endpoint from the same ledger. A resolved real quest replaces
its slot; it is never an extra line of earned XP. Scope consumption and replacement to the correct
character, faction and release.

Keep expected mandatory combat as the owner's natural planning reserve where measurements are
absent. Do not add a fabricated fixed buffer, apply a second combat bonus, or require every grind
checkpoint to be timed before authoring the route. Where compatible explicit combat is available,
count it once; combat already reflected in an actual XP bar cannot be awarded again. Actual party
bars, legal access and known quest restrictions still govern live readiness. Supply a specific
nearby catch-up option only when actual progress is short, without discarding accepted chains or
required future unlocks.

### Verification and finish lines

Add focused tests for four independent slots per faction, partial and complete ID discovery,
known extra quests, class restrictions, duplicate mappings, authoritative zero rewards, exactly-once
scaling, quest-level versus hand-in-level handling, level-60 XP, and persisted-plan migration.
Include integration tests proving that all three formerly empty dungeon bundles contribute to the
planning forecast while unidentified slots remain absent from executable quest directives. Test
the actual-XP checkpoint path, stale build/evidence rejection and replacement without losing saves.

Run package tests, strict typechecks and lint first, then rebuild the full supported browser/addon
matrix and inspect the resulting continuous per-member XP ledgers. Rebuild the existing 33 Alliance
browser editions and 28 exact addon editions; enumerate the implemented Horde matrix explicitly
rather than treating Alliance results as proof for Horde. Check desktop/mobile rendering, save/resume
and matching browser/addon endpoints. Preserve one reproducible report bound to code commit,
source archive, runtime build, bundle configuration and reward/evidence checksums.

The first finish line is a complete **planning guide**: all applicable dungeons included, guaranteed
quest counts respected, known and estimated XP visible, continuous per-member funding calculated,
and actionable instructions for all identified content. Missing IDs do not block that finish line,
but unidentified quest details must remain visibly pending. Do not claim the entire catalog was
fetched merely because the current crawl queue completed.

The second finish line is an **executable guide for the supported content**: real pickup/objective/
hand-in instructions, current access/eligibility checks and a successful bounded addon review. It
can ship with transparently estimated XP and actual-party checkpoints; it cannot claim exact
instructions for unidentified quests or a fully measured optimal 1–60 time.

Keep measured reward/combat/timing certification separate. The pre-bundle backlog had 26 historical
reward estimates, 112 quest-validation task/code pairs and 105 grind-measurement tasks. The new
bundle audit has zero blocking tasks, 147 validation tasks and 106 measurement tasks; it still uses
26 historical route rewards from the frozen published snapshot. The completed-quest audit supersedes
that reward count: zero historical hand-ins, 121 validation tasks and 106 measurement tasks remain.
These are evidence requirements, not blocking bugs or a demand for one recording per task. Batch
tests only when their build and character/party contexts genuinely match. Inaccessible beta
content remains explicitly untested, without holding the planning product hostage to future IDs.

The bundle identity/replacement, reward ledger, route consumers and audit are now implemented and
verified locally. The fresh rewards, source report, 33-edition audit, 28-edition addon matrix and
targeted in-game checklist have now been rebuilt. For a later executable/distributed release
(currently deferred): perform the bounded in-game review and authorized release, plus explicit
review of the remaining static source differences. These do not hold up the approved code-only
planning finish line. The observation
consumer is now implemented and tested; actual observations and source approval remain separate.
Do not expand to an unrelated full-archive crawl as
a release prerequisite, and do not claim a deployment until a pushed release is actually activated.
